\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot F_{2}(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,\eta_{1}\,k\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\cdot H^{Inc}_{1}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx+Error_{1}.

By the use of Taylor expansions for
H_{1}^{Inc}(\cdot),\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we obtain

(6.21)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\mathcal{K}\cdot\int_{D_{1}}F_{1}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,k^{4}\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\,dx\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot\int_{D_{2}}F_{2}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,\eta_{1}\,k\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\,dx\cdot H^{Inc}_{1}(z_{1})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,k^{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\,dx\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle Error_{1}+Error_{2},

where
Error_{2}
admits the following estimation

\displaystyle Error_{2}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,d^{-4}\,\left\|\tilde{F_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\left\|\tilde{A_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\ a^{2}\,\left\|\tilde{A_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,d^{-3}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E_{2}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\left\|\tilde{A_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

Besides, thanks to Friedrichs Inequality, see
[
32
, Corollary 3.2]
, we know that

(6.22)

\left\|\tilde{F_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\;\lesssim\;\left\|Curl\left(\tilde{F_{2}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\tilde{A_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\;\lesssim\;\left\|Curl\left(\tilde{A_{1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})},

which, by scaling back the rotational operator, gives us

(6.23)

\left\|\tilde{F_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\;\lesssim\;a\,\left\|\widetilde{Curl\left(F_{2}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\overset{(\ref{def-F_j})}{=}\;a\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{E}_{2})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})},

and

\left\|\tilde{A_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\;\lesssim\;a\,\left\|\widetilde{Curl\left(A_{1}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\;\overset{(\ref{P1W1})}{=}\;a\;\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}.

Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle Error_{2}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{7}d^{-4}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\,a^{3}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{6}d^{-3}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right),

which, by using Proposition
2.7
, can be reduced to

(6.24)

Error_{2}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{8-h}\,d^{-4}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4-h}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{7-h}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right).

In addition, by knowing that

\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\cdot\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\right)\cdot\mathcal{K}=I,

setting

(6.25)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(1)}}:=\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\cdot\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\right)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}A_{1}(x)\,dx\,\in\,\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3},

and using
(\ref{Qj1})

we rewrite
(\ref{AL-prime1})
, after multiplication of its both sides by
\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\cdot\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\right)
, as

(6.26)

Q_{1}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(1)}}\cdot\left(k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot Q_{2}+\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times R_{2}\right)\,=\,i\,k\,\eta_{1}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(1)}}\cdot H^{Inc}_{1}(z_{1})\,+\,Error_{1}\,+\,Error_{2}.

Moreover, following a similar argument to
[
9
, Appendix A.1, Formula (A.30)]
, we can know that

(6.27)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(1)}}\;=\;\frac{a^{5-h}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\;{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}\;+\;\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\right),

where
{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}
is the tensor given by

{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}\,:=\,\int_{B_{1}}\phi_{n_{0},B_{1}}(y)\,dy\,\otimes\,\int_{B_{1}}\phi_{n_{0},B_{1}}(y)\,dy.

Then, by plugging
(\ref{express-A1-mu})
into
(\ref{la-Q1})
, we deduce that

(6.28)

Q_{1}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\frac{a^{5-h}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\;{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}\cdot\left(k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot Q_{2}+\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times R_{2}\right)\,=\,i\,k\,\eta_{1}\,\frac{a^{5-h}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\;{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}\cdot H^{Inc}_{1}(z_{1})\,+Error_{3},

where

Error_{3}\,:=\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,a^{5}\,\left(k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot Q_{2}\,+\,\nabla\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times R_{2}\right)\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k\,\eta_{1}\,a^{5}\right)\,+\,Error_{1}\,+\,Error_{2}.

To estimate the above term, we start by taking the modulus on its both sides and we use
(\ref{def-eta}),(\ref{Qj1})
and
(\ref{F2aP1E2})
, to obtain

\displaystyle\left|Error_{3}\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{7}\,d^{-3}\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,+\,a^{6}\,d^{-2}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,+\,a^{3}\,+\,\left|Error_{1}\right|\,+\,\left|Error_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{Est-Err-1})}{\underset{(\ref{Est-Err-2})}{\lesssim}}

a^{\min\left(7-3t;8-h-4t\right)}\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}+a^{\min(4;7-h-3t)}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}+a^{2}\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}+a^{3}

To finish with the estimation of
Error_{3}
, we use the a-prior estimates presented in Proposition
2.5
, under the conditions
t\,<\,1
and
h\,<\,1
, we can obtain

Error_{3}\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(3;7-2h-3t)}\right).

Regarding the estimation of
Error_{3}
and the scale of
\eta_{1}
, see
(\ref{def-eta})
, the R.H.S of (
6.28
) is of order
a^{3-h}
that dominates. This justifies the first equation in (
1.15
).

(2)

The algebraic system with respect to
\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)
.

By integrating the equation
(\ref{LS-D1-2})
, over
D_{1}
, using
(\ref{def-Vm}),(\ref{grad-M-1st})
and
(\ref{RefNeeded1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\int_{D_{1}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot E_{1}^{Inc}(x)\,dx\,+\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx.

Then, by taking the Taylor expansion of
\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
at
(z_{1},z_{2})
, we obtain

(6.29)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{1}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\,dx\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot E_{1}^{Inc}(x)\,dx\,+\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,J_{6}\,+\,k^{2}\,J_{7},

where

\displaystyle J_{6}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{1}+t(x-z_{1}),y)\cdot\mathcal{P}(x,z_{1})\,dt\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx,

\displaystyle J_{7}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(x,z_{2}+t(y-z_{2}))\cdot\mathcal{P}(y,z_{2})\,dt\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx.

Next, we estimate
J_{6}
and
J_{7}
.

(a)

Estimation of
J_{6}
.

(6.30)

\displaystyle\left|J_{6}\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left|\eta_{1}\right|\,\left|\eta_{2}\right|\left\lVert V_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}\,\left\lVert\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{1}+t(\cdot-z_{1}),y)\cdot\mathcal{P}(\cdot,z_{1})\,dt\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle a^{5}\,d^{-4}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}.

(b)

Estimation of
J_{7}
.

Similarly, as
J_{7}
’s expression is almost identical to
J_{6}
’s expression, we deduce from
(\ref{es-J6})
,

(6.31)

J_{7}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,d^{-4}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right).

In addition to the estimation of
J_{6}
and
J_{7}
, we need to analyze the second term on the R.H.S of (
6.29
), that we denote by
J_{5}
. For
J_{5}
, by using the expression (
3.1
), there holds

J_{5}\,=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\underset{y}{Curl}\left(F_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx\,=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\times F_{2}(y)\,dy\,dx.

By further expanding
\nabla\Phi_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
at
(z_{1},z_{2})
, we obtain

(6.32)

J_{5}\,=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\,dx\,\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times\int_{D_{2}}\,F_{2}(y)\,dy\,+\,k^{2}\,J_{8}\,+\,k^{2}\,J_{9},

where

\displaystyle J_{8}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}\right)\left(z_{1}+t(x-z_{1}),z_{2}\right)\cdot(x-z_{1})\,dt\times F_{2}(y)\,dy\,dx,

\displaystyle J_{9}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\left(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}\right)\left(z_{1},z_{2}+t(y-z_{2})\right)\cdot(y-z_{2})\,dt\times F_{2}(y)\,dy\,dx.

Next, we estimate
J_{8}
and
J_{9}
.

(a)

Estimation of
J_{8}
.

(6.33)

\displaystyle\left|J_{8}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{1}\right|\,\left|\eta_{2}\right|\left\lVert V_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}\,\left\lVert\int_{D_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}\right)\left(z_{1}+t(\cdot-z_{1}),z_{2}\right)\cdot(\cdot-z_{1})\,dt\times F_{2}(y)\,dy\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,a^{2}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert V_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert F_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right),

where the last estimation is justified by the use of Friedrichs Inequality, see
(\ref{FI})
.

(b)

Estimation of
J_{9}
.

Similarly, as
J_{9}
’s expression is almost identical to
J_{8}
’s expression, we deduce, from
(\ref{es-J8})
,

J_{9}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right).

Hence, the term
J_{5}
, given by
(\ref{J5})
, becomes,

(6.34)

J_{5}\,=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\,dx\,\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times\int_{D_{2}}\,F_{2}(y)\,dy\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right).

Consequently, by using
(\ref{es-J6}),(\ref{es-J7})
and
(\ref{J5=})
, the equation
(\ref{eq-P31-mid2})
takes the following form,

(6.35)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{1}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{1}}\cdot\left[\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(y)\,dy\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times\int_{D_{2}}\,F_{2}(y)\,dy\,\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{1}}\cdot E_{1}^{Inc}(z_{1})\,+\,Error^{(2)}_{1},

where, we have used a Taylor expansion for the incident field to obtain,

(6.36)

\displaystyle Error^{(2)}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\nabla E_{1}^{Inc}(z_{1}+t(x-z_{1}))\cdot(x-z_{1})\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,d^{-4}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right),

and we have denoted by
\bm{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{1}}
the following notation

\bm{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{1}}\;:=\;\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\,dx.

Obviously, the first term on the R.H.S of
(\ref{J10})
, admits the following estimation

(6.37)

J_{10}\;=\;\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{1}}V_{1}(x)\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\nabla E_{1}^{Inc}(z_{1}+t(x-z_{1}))\cdot(x-z_{1})\,dt\,dx\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(\,a^{2}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

Now, for
\bm{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{1}}
, after scaling from
D_{1}
to
B_{1}
, the following expansion holds,

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(2)}}\,=\,a^{3}\int_{B_{1}}\tilde{V}_{1}(x)\,dx\,\overset{(\ref{RefNeeded1})}{=}\,a^{3}\sum_{n}\left\langle\tilde{V}_{1},e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx,

which, by using
(\ref{V1-en3})
, becomes,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(2)}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{1}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\right)}\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\eta_{1}\,\sum_{n}\frac{}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{1}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\right)}\left\langle\tilde{V}_{1},\mathcal{S}^{ka}\left(e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx,

where
\mathcal{S}^{ka}(e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)})
is given by (
5.62
). Then, thanks to
(\ref{EstSka})
and
(\ref{es-V1})
, we can estimate the second term on the R.H.S to obtain

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(2)}}\,\overset{\eqref{es-V12}}{=}\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{1}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\right)}\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,a^{7}\right),

and, by multiplying its both sides by
\eta_{1}
and using
\eta_{1}
’s expression, given by
(\ref{def-eta})
, we obtain

(6.38)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(2)}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{0}\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\left(\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\,+\,a^{2}\right)}\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,a^{5}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})}\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{a^{2}}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\,\left(\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\,+\,a^{2}\right)}\,\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx+\mathcal{O}\,\left(k^{2}\,a^{5}\right).

Clearly, the second term on the R.H.S can be estimated as,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{n}\frac{a^{3}\,a^{2}}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\,\left(\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})\,+\,a^{2}\right)}\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{5}\,\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle I,e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\right).

Hence, by plugging the above estimation into
(\ref{Equa1124})
, we deduce

(6.39)

\eta_{1}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{(2)}}\,=\,\,a^{3}\,{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\right),

where

{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\,:=\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})}\,\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx.

This implies, by returning to
(\ref{eq-P31-mid2})
and using
(\ref{eta1A12=})
, together with the notations
(\ref{Qj1})
,

R_{1}\,-\,k^{2}\,a^{3}\,{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\cdot\left[\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot R_{2}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times Q_{2}\,\right]\,=\,a^{3}\,{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\cdot E_{1}^{Inc}(z_{1})\,+\,Error^{(2)}_{2},

where
Error^{(2)}_{2}
being given by

Error^{(2)}_{2}\,:=\,Error^{(2)}_{1}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,\left[\Upsilon_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\cdot R_{2}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{1},z_{2})\times Q_{2}\,\right]\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\right),

where
Error^{(2)}_{1}
is defined by
(\ref{J10})
.
We need to estimate
Error^{(2)}_{2}
. To do this, by taking the modulus on the both sides we obtain

\left|Error^{(2)}_{2}\right|\,\lesssim\,\left|Error^{(2)}_{1}\right|\,+\,a^{8}\,\left[d^{-3}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,+\,d^{-2}\,\left\|\tilde{F}_{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\right]\,+\,a^{5},

hence, by using
(\ref{es-J10}),(\ref{J10}),(\ref{F2aP1E2})
and the a-priori estimates presented by Proposition
2.5
and Proposition
2.7
, we deduce

Error^{(2)}_{2}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{7-h}\,d^{-4}\right).

To investigate the linear algebraic system in
D_{2}
, we multiply each side of
(\ref{LS-es})
by
\eta_{2}
, and take the inverse of the operator
\bm{T}_{k,2}
, to get

(6.40)

\eta_{2}\,E_{2}(x)\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\bm{T}_{k,2}^{-1}\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot E_{1}(y)\,dy\,=\,\eta_{2}\,\bm{T}_{k,2}^{-1}\left(E_{2}^{Inc}\right)(x),\quad x\in D_{2}.

(1)

The algebraic system with respect to
\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)
.

Then, by multiplying the both sides of (
6.40
) by
\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2}))
, with
x\in D_{2}
, integrating over
D_{2}
and using
(\ref{AI1})
, we get

\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\right)\cdot E_{2}(x)\,dx\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}W_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot E_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx\,=\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}W_{2}(x)\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(x)\,dx,

which can be rewritten as

(6.41)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\cdot\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(x)dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle k^{2}\eta_{2}\eta_{1}\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)dydx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(x)dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle k^{2}\eta_{2}\eta_{1}\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)dydx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle k^{2}L_{1}+L_{2}+k^{2}L_{3},

where we have used
(\ref{grad-M-1st}),(\ref{prop-es-W})
and the fact that

\int_{D_{2}}\overset{2}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot E_{2}^{inc}(x)\,dx\,=\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\right)\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(x)\,dx\,=\,0.

Besides we have used the following notations,

\displaystyle L_{1}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle L_{2}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle L_{3}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx.

Next, we estimate
L_{1},L_{2}
and
L_{3}
.

(a)

Estimation of
L_{1}
.

By Taylor expansion for
\Phi_{k}(x,\cdot)
and the relation
(\ref{RefNeeded1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{1}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,z_{1}+t(y-z_{1}))\cdot(y-z_{1})\,dt\,\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

(6.42)

\displaystyle\left|L_{1}\right|\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,a^{5}\,d^{-2}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\underset{(\ref{max-P1P3})}{\overset{(\ref{prop-es-W})}{=}}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{11-2h}d^{-2}\right).

(b)

Estimation of
L_{2}
.

For
L_{2}
, we directly have

(6.43)

\left|L_{2}\right|\,\leq\,\left|\eta_{2}\right|\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\,\left\lVert E_{2}^{Inc}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\,\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{=}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{3}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right)\,\overset{(\ref{prop-es-W})}{=}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{8-h}\right).

(c)

Estimation of
L_{3}
.

For
L_{3}
, we directly get by using the Calderón-Zygmund inequality that

(6.44)

\left|L_{3}\right|\,\lesssim\,a\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,\underset{(\ref{max-P1P3})}{\overset{(\ref{prop-es-W})}{=}}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{8-h}\right).

Combining
(\ref{MLEqua0919}),(\ref{es-L2})
and (
6.44
), for
h<1
, it allows us to write (
6.41
) as

(6.45)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\cdot\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{8-h}\right).

For the above equation, we need to analyze the terms containing
\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)
. To do this, we recall
(\ref{def-F_j})
that we have

(6.46)

\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(W_{2}\right)\,=\,Curl\left(A_{2}\right),\quad\mbox{with}\quad\mathrm{div}\,\left(A_{2}\right)\,=\,0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu\times A_{2}=0.

Then, by using
(\ref{W2A2})
and
(\ref{def-F_j})
in
(\ref{mid-D2-1})
, with help of integration by parts, we end up with the following equation,

(6.47)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\mathcal{K}\cdot\int_{D_{2}}F_{2}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot H^{Inc}_{2}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{8-h}\right),

where
\mathcal{K}
is the matrix given by
(\ref{def-K})
. Now, by expanding
\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\nabla\Phi_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
, at
(z_{2},z_{1})
, we derive from (
6.47
) the following equation

(6.48)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\mathcal{K}\cdot\int_{D_{2}}F_{2}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot\int_{D_{1}}F_{1}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times\int_{D_{1}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,L_{4}\,+\,L_{5}\,+\,L_{6}\,+\,L_{7}\,+\,L_{8}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{8-h}\right).

Next, we set and we estimate the terms
L_{4},\cdots,L_{8}
.

(a)

Estimation of
L_{4}
.

\displaystyle L_{4}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}),z_{1})\cdot\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\,dt\cdot F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx,

\displaystyle\left|L_{4}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{1}\right|\left|\eta_{2}\right|\left\lVert A_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\left\lVert\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{2}+t(\cdot-z_{2}),z_{1})\cdot\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\,dt\cdot F_{1}(y)\,dy\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a^{2}d^{-4}\left\lVert A_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\left\lVert F_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})},

which, after scaling from
D_{j}
to
B_{j}
, with
j=1,2
, and using the Friedrichs Inequality, we obtain

(6.49)

L_{4}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{7}\,d^{-4}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

(b)

Estimation of
L_{5}
. Following the same argument, we have

\displaystyle L_{5}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{2},z_{1}+t(y-z_{1}))\cdot\mathcal{P}(y,z_{1})\,dt\cdot F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx,

(6.50)

\displaystyle|L_{5}|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a^{7}d^{-4}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}.

(c)

Estimation of
J_{6}
.

\displaystyle L_{6}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}))\cdot(x-z_{2})\,dt\,dx,

(6.51)

\displaystyle|L_{6}|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{2}\right|\,\left\lVert A_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2}+t(\cdot-z_{2}))\cdot(\cdot-z_{2})\,dt\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\overset{(\ref{EincHinc})}{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle a^{4}\,\left\lVert\tilde{A}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right),

where the last estimation is justified by the use of Friedrichs Inequality.

(d)

Estimation of
J_{7}
.

(6.52)

\displaystyle L_{7}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k})(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}),z_{1})\cdot(x-z_{2})\,dt\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx,

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\lesssim}a^{2}d^{-3}\left\lVert A_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{1})}\lesssim a^{6}d^{-3}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}.

(e)

Estimation of
L_{8}
.

L_{8}\,:=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k})(z_{2},z_{1}+t(y-z_{1}))\cdot(y-z_{1})\,dt\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx.

Observe that
L_{8}
’s expression is almost identical to
L_{7}
’s expression. Hence, from the above estimation, we deduce that

(6.53)

L_{8}\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

Finally, by gathering
(\ref{EstL4}),(\ref{EstL5}),(\ref{EstL6}),(\ref{EstL7})
and
(\ref{EstL8})
, we deduce that with (
1.5
),

(6.54)

L_{4}+\cdots+L_{8}\,\underset{(\ref{max-P1P3})}{\overset{(\ref{prop-es-W})}{=}}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(9-h-4t;6)}\right).

By returning to
(\ref{mid4-D2-P1})
, using
(\ref{EstL4+...+L8})
and
(\ref{Qj1})
, we deduce

(6.55)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\cdot Q_{2}\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{4}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot H_{2}^{Inc}(z_{2})\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(9-h-4t;6)}\right).

Similarly to (
6.25
), by denoting

(6.56)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(1)}}:=\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\cdot\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\right)\cdot\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx,

and multiplying the both sides of
(
6.55
) by
\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\cdot\mathcal{K}\cdot\mathcal{K}^{\perp}\right)
, we obtain

(6.57)

Q_{2}-k^{2}\eta_{2}\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(1)}}\cdot\left[k^{2}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}+\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}\right]=ik\eta_{2}\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(1)}}\cdot H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(9-h-4t;6)}\right).

Now, we need to evaluate the tensor
\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(1)}}
given by
(\ref{def-A21})
. Indeed, by scaling from
D_{2}
to
B_{2}
and using the fact that
I\,=-\,Curl\left(\mathcal{Q}(x)\right)
, where
\mathcal{Q}(x)
is the matrix defined by

(6.58)

\mathcal{Q}(x)\,:=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&x_{3}&0\\
0&0&x_{1}\\
x_{2}&0&0\end{pmatrix},

we obtain

\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\;=-\;a^{3}\,\int_{B_{2}}\tilde{A}_{2}(x)\cdot Curl\mathcal{Q}(x)\,dx,

which, by integration by parts and the fact that
Curl\left(\tilde{A}_{2}\right)\,=\,a\,\widetilde{Curl\left(A_{2}\right)}
, implies

\displaystyle\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,a^{4}\,\int_{B_{2}}\widetilde{CurlA_{2}}(x)\cdot\mathcal{Q}(x)\,dx\,\overset{(\ref{W2A2})}{=}-\,a^{4}\,\int_{B_{2}}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{W}_{2}\right)(x)\cdot\mathcal{Q}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a^{4}\,\sum_{n}\left\langle\tilde{W}_{2},e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\cdot\mathcal{Q}(x)\,dx\right)^{Tr},

By keeping the dominant term of
\left\langle\tilde{W}_{2},e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}
, see for instance
(\ref{Equa1036})
, and using
(\ref{pre-cond})
, we obtain

(6.59)

\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\,=\,a^{5}\,\sum_{n}\left\langle\mathcal{P}(\cdot,0),e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx\right)^{Tr}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,(a^{7}),

due to the fact that

\displaystyle a^{4}\sum_{n}\left|k^{2}a^{2}\eta_{2}\left\langle N_{B_{2}}^{-ka}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{W}_{2})+\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{W}_{2})\right),e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\otimes\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\cdot\mathcal{Q}(x)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a^{6}\left(\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{W}_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}+\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{W}_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right)\overset{\eqref{prop-es-W}}{\lesssim}a^{7}.

Since there holds

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{P}(\cdot,0),e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{pre-cond}}{=}

\displaystyle\,\left\langle\mathcal{P}(\cdot,0),Curl\left(\phi_{n,B_{2}}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left\langle Curl\left(\mathcal{P}(\cdot,0)\right),\phi_{n,B_{2}}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\overset{\eqref{def-K}}{=}\,\left\langle\mathcal{K},\phi_{n,B_{2}}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})},

then, (
6.59
) indicates,

\int_{D_{2}}A_{2}(x)\,dx\,=\,a^{5}\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle\mathcal{K},\phi_{n,B_{2}}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx\right)^{Tr}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,(a^{7}).

Hence,
(\ref{def-A21})
takes the following form,

(6.60)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(1)}}\,=\,a^{5}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,(a^{7}),

where the tensor
{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}
is given by

{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\,:=\,\sum_{n}\,\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx\,\otimes\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx.

Then, by plugging
(\ref{es-A21})
into
(\ref{mid6})
, we get

(6.61)

\displaystyle Q_{2}\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,a^{5}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\cdot\left[k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}\right]=\,i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,a^{5}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\cdot H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,a^{7}\,\left[k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}\right]\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(9-h-4t;6)}\right).

We need to estimate the second term on the R.H.S of the above equation.

\displaystyle\left|\cdots\right|\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\left|k^{2}\,a^{7}\,\left[k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{7}\,\left[d^{-3}\,\left|Q_{1}\right|\,+\,d^{-2}\,\left|R_{1}\right|\right]\,\underset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\overset{(\ref{Qj1})}{\lesssim}}\,a^{8}\,\left[d^{-3}\,\left\|\tilde{F}_{1}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,+\,d^{-2}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right],

which, by using Friedrichs Inequality, gives us

\left|\cdots\right|\,\lesssim\,\,a^{8}\,\left[d^{-3}\,a\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,+\,d^{-2}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right]\,\overset{(\ref{max-P1P3})}{=}\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{10-h-3t}\right).

Then, by plugging the above estimation into
(\ref{Equa0748})
, we obtain

\displaystyle Q_{2}\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,a^{5}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\cdot\left[k^{2}\,\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot Q_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times R_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,\eta_{2}\,a^{5}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\cdot H^{Inc}_{2}(z_{2})+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(9-h-4t;6)}\right).

The R.H.S of the above equation makes sens under the condition

4\,-\,h\,-\,4t\,>\,0.

(2)

The algebraic system with respect to
\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)
.

By integrating
(\ref{LS-D2-3})
over
D_{2}
and using the definition of

V_{2}
, given by
(\ref{def-Vm})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(x)\,dx\,+\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\int_{D_{1}}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\times F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx,

where, on the R.H.S, we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}\Upsilon_{k}(x,y)\cdot\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,\overset{(\ref{grad-M-1st})}{=}\,\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-F_j})}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,Curl\left(F_{1}\right)(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\times F_{1}(y)\,dy.

Hence, by taking the Taylor expansion of
\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\Phi_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
, at
(z_{2},z_{1})
, we have

(6.62)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{2}\right)(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(z_{2})\,+\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\,dx\cdot\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times\int_{D_{1}}F_{1}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L_{9}+L_{10}+L_{11}+L_{12}+L_{13},

where
L_{9},\cdots,L_{13}
are defined and evaluated as follows.

(a)

Estimation of
L_{9}
.

\displaystyle L_{9}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}),z_{1})\cdot\mathcal{P}(x,z_{2})\,dt\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx

(6.63)

\displaystyle L_{9}\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{=}

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}d^{-4}\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

(b)

Estimation of
L_{10}
.

L_{10}\,:=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k})(z_{2},z_{1}+t(y-z_{1}))\cdot\mathcal{P}(y,z_{1})\,dt\cdot\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{1}\right)(y)\,dy\,dx.

It is easy to see that
L_{10}
’s expression is almost identical to
L_{9}
’s expression. Hence, from
(\ref{Est-L9})
, we get

(6.64)

L_{10}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{5}d^{-4}\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

(c)

Estimation of
L_{11}
.

(6.65)

L_{11}\,:=\,\eta_{2}\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}E_{2}^{Inc}(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}))\cdot(x-z_{2})\,dt\,dx\underset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\overset{(\ref{EincHinc})}{=}}\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right).

(d)

Estimation of
L_{12}
.

(6.66)

\displaystyle L_{12}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}\right)(z_{2}+t(x-z_{2}),z_{1})\cdot(x-z_{2})\,dt\times F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{def-eta})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,a^{5}\,d^{-3}\,\left\|\tilde{V}_{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\left\|\tilde{F}_{1}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right),

where the last estimation is a consequence of the Friederichs Inequality.

(e)

Estimation of
L_{13}
.

L_{13}\,:=\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,\eta_{2}\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\cdot\int_{D_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\left(\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}\right)(z_{2},z_{1}+t(y-z_{1}))\cdot(y-z_{1})\,dt\times F_{1}(y)\,dy\,dx.

Observe that
L_{13}
’s expression is almost identical to
L_{12}
’s expression. Hence, from
(\ref{Est-L12})
, we deduce that

(6.67)

L_{13}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{6}\,d^{-3}\,\left\lVert\tilde{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tilde{E}_{1}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\right).

Finally, by gathering
(\ref{Est-L9}),(\ref{Est-L10}),(\ref{Est-L11}),(\ref{Est-L12}),(\ref{Est-L13})
, and using
(\ref{es-V12})
and
(\ref{max-P1P3})
, we deduce

(6.68)

L_{9}+\cdots+L_{13}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4-h}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{7-3t-2h}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{7-h-4t}\right).

Hence, by returning to
(\ref{LS-P32-expan})
, using the estimation
(\ref{Est-L9+...+L13})
and the notations
(\ref{Qj1})
, with

(6.69)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\,:=\,\int_{D_{2}}V_{2}(x)\,dx,

we obtain

(6.70)

R_{2}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\cdot\left[\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot R_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times Q_{1}\right]\,=\,\eta_{2}\,\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(z_{2})\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min(4-h;7-3t-2h;7-h-4t}\right).

We need to investigate
\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}
. To do this, in
(\ref{A22V2})
, by scaling from
D_{2}
to
B_{2}
, we expand
\tilde{V}_{2}
into the basis
\left(e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, with
(\ref{V2-project})
to get

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{\eta_{2}}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\,\left\langle\tilde{V}_{2},\mathcal{S}^{ka}\left(e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\otimes\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx,

with
\mathcal{S}^{ka}(e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)})
given by (
5.62
). Next, we estimate the second term on the R.H.S.

\displaystyle\left|\cdots\right|\;

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\;\left|a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,\frac{\eta_{2}}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\,\left\langle\tilde{V}_{2},\mathcal{S}^{ka}\left(e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\otimes\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{condition-on-k})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\;a^{3-h}\,\sum_{n}\,\left|\left\langle\tilde{V}_{2},\mathcal{S}^{ka}\left(e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right|\,\left|\left\langle I,e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{3-h}\;\left\|\mathcal{S}^{-ka}\left(\tilde{V}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\overset{(\ref{EstSka})}{\lesssim}\,a^{5-h}\;\left\|\tilde{V}_{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\overset{(\ref{es-V12})}{=}\left(a^{5-2h}\right).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\,\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{5-2h}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{condition-on-k})}{=}

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{a^{3-h}}{d_{0}}\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{*},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{*},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle a^{3}\,\sum_{n\neq n_{*}}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\,\left(a^{5-2h}\right).

Obviously, regarding the second term, there holds

\displaystyle\left|\cdots\right|\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\left|a^{3}\sum_{n\neq n_{*}}\frac{1}{\left(1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{2})\right)}\,\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\otimes\left(\int_{B_{2}}e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{3}\,\sum_{n\neq n_{*}}\left|\left\langle e_{n,B_{2}}^{(3)},I\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\right|^{2}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{3}\right).

Hence, the tensor
\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}
becomes,

(6.71)

\bm{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{(2)}}\,=\,\frac{a^{3-h}}{\pm d_{0}}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{3}\right),\quad\mbox{for}\quad h<1,

where the tensor
{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}
is given by

{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\,:=\,\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{*},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\;\otimes\;\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{*},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx.

Then, by plugging
(\ref{A22P022})
into
(\ref{Equa1336})
, we deduce that

R_{2}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{2}\,\frac{a^{3-h}}{\pm d_{0}}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\cdot\left[\Upsilon_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\cdot R_{1}\,+\,\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(z_{2},z_{1})\times Q_{1}\right]\,=\,\eta_{2}\,\frac{a^{3-h}}{\pm d_{0}}\,{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\cdot E_{2}^{Inc}(z_{2})\,+\,\widetilde{Error^{**}},

where, by using
(\ref{def-eta}),(\ref{max-P1P3})
and the Friedrichs Inequality, we have that

\widetilde{Error^{**}}\,:=\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\min\left(4-h;7-3t-2h;7-h-4t;6-3t\right)}\right).

The proof of Proposition
3.1
is now complete.

7.
Appendix

7.1.
Lorentz model

Here we show that the conditions
(\ref{def-eta})
and
(\ref{condition-on-k})
can be derived from the Lorentz model by choosing appropriate incident frequency
k
. Indeed, recall the Lorentz model for the relative permittivity that

(7.1)

\epsilon_{r}=1+\dfrac{k_{\mathrm{p}}^{2}}{k_{0}^{2}-k^{2}-ik\xi},

where
k_{\mathrm{p}}
is the plasmonic frequency,
k_{0}
is the undamped frequency resonance of the background and
\xi
is the damping frequency with
\xi\,\ll\,1
. In
D_{m}
, with
m=1,2
, we respectively denote
\xi_{m}
as the damping frequencies,
k_{0,m}
as the undamped frequencies, and
k_{p,m}
as the according plasmonic frequencies. Let

k_{0,1}\,:=\,k_{0},\quad k_{p,1}\,=\,k_{p},\quad\mbox{in}\;D_{1},

and
k_{0,2}
,
k_{p,2}
such that

(7.2)

k_{0,2}^{2}\,<\,k^{2}_{0,1}\,<\,k_{0,2}^{2}\,+\,k_{p,2}^{2}\quad\mbox{in}\;D_{2}.

(1)

Regarding the dielectric resonance.
If the frequency
k
is chosen to be real and
k^{2}
close to the undamped resonance frequency
k^{2}_{0,1}
in the format that

(7.3)

k^{2}\,-\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,=\,-\,\frac{k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}}{\left(1\mp{\mathrm{Re\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{h}\right)\pm\dfrac{{\mathrm{Im\,}}^{2}(c_{0})\,a^{h}}{\left(1\mp{\mathrm{Re\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{h}\right)}-k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})}=\,-\,k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,\left[1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right],

and

k\;\xi_{1}\,=\,\pm\,\frac{{\mathrm{Im\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{2}\,(k^{2}-k^{2}_{0,1})}{\left(1\mp{\mathrm{Re\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{h}\right)}\,=\,\mp\,{\mathrm{Im\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{4}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,k_{p,1}^{2}\left[1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right],

where
\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})
is an eigenvalue of the Newtonian potential operator
N_{B_{1}}(\cdot)
. Then, there holds,

{\mathrm{Re\,}}\Big(\eta_{1}\Big)\,=\,a^{-2}\,\Big(\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\Big)^{-1}\left(1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathrm{Im\,}}\Big(\eta_{1}\Big)\,=\,\pm\,{\mathrm{Im\,}}\Big(c_{0}\Big)\,\Big(\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\Big)^{-1}+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4}\right),

which implies that
D_{1}
behaves as a dielectric nano-particle. Furthermore, by choosing
k
satisfying
(\ref{assumo-k-k0})
, we derive that

1\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{1}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,=\,\pm\,c_{0}\,a^{h},

see
(\ref{condition-on-k})
. For more details we refer the readers to
[
9
, Remark 1.1 & Remark 1.2]
.

(2)

Regarding the plasmonic resonance. Using the introduced notations, we start by recalling the Lorentz model for the permittivity related to the nano-particle
D_{2}
, given by
(\ref{Lorentz model})
,

\epsilon_{r}^{(2)}\,=\,1\,+\,\frac{k_{p,2}^{2}}{k_{0,2}^{2}-k^{2}-ik\xi_{2}}\,\overset{(\ref{assumo-k-k0})}{=}\,1+\frac{k_{p,2}^{2}}{k_{0,2}^{2}-\left(k_{0,1}^{2}\,-\,k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,\left[1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right]\right)\,-\,i\,k\,\xi_{2}}.

Hence,

Re\left(\epsilon_{r}^{(2)}\right)\,=\,1\,+\,\,\frac{k_{p,2}^{2}\left[k_{0,2}^{2}\,-\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,+\,k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,\left[1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right]\right]}{\left[k_{0,2}^{2}-k_{0,1}^{2}\,+\,k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})\,k_{0,1}^{2}\,\left[1\,+\,\mathcal{O}(a^{h})\right]\right]^{2}\,+\,\left(k\,\xi_{2}\right)^{2}}.

Besides, by using
(\ref{assump2-k})
and the fact that
\xi_{2}\,\ll\,1
, we can deduce that

Re\left(\epsilon_{r}^{(2)}\right)\,<\,0.

Then
D_{2}
behaves like a plasmonic nano-particle. Furthermore, by choosing
k
such that

(7.4)

k^{2}\,=\,k^{2}_{0,2}\,+\,\frac{\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,k^{2}_{p,2}\,\left(1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,Re\left(d_{0}\right)\right)}{\left|1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,d_{0}\right|^{2}},

and letting
\xi_{2}
to be given by

\xi_{2}\,=\,\pm\,\frac{a^{h}\,Im\left(d_{0}\right)\,\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,k_{p,2}^{2}}{\left|1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,d_{0}\right|\,\sqrt{k^{2}_{0,2}\,\left|1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,d_{0}\right|^{2}\,+\,\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,k^{2}_{p,2}\,\left|1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,d_{0}\right|}},

we deduce that

1\,+\,\eta_{2}\,\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,=\,\pm\,d_{0}\,a^{h},

see
(\ref{condition-on-k})
.

According to the analysis above, there is a frequency
k
that can be considered as a dielectric resonance and a plasmonic resonance under certain conditions. Indeed, by equating
(\ref{assumo-k-k0})
and
(\ref{Equa0551})
, we obtain

k_{0,1}^{2}\left[1\,-\,\frac{k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})}{\left(1\mp{\mathrm{Re\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{h}\right)\pm\dfrac{{\mathrm{Im\,}}^{2}(c_{0})\,a^{h}}{\left(1\mp{\mathrm{Re\,}}(c_{0})\,a^{h}\right)}-k_{p,1}^{2}\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B_{1})}\right]\,=\,k^{2}_{0,2}\,+\,\frac{\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,k^{2}_{p,2}\,\left(1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,Re\left(d_{0}\right)\right)}{\left|1\,\mp\,a^{h}\,d_{0}\right|^{2}},

which, by keeping only the dominant terms, gives us the following condition

(7.5)

k_{0,1}^{2}\,=\,k_{0,2}^{2}\,+\,\lambda_{n_{\star}}^{(3)}(B_{2})\,k_{p,2}^{2},

establishing a correlation between the Lorentz model parameters associated with the dielectric nano-particle
D_{1}
, i.e.
k_{0,1}
, and the parameters associated with the plasmonic nano-particle
D_{2}
, i.e.
k_{0,2}
and
k_{p,2}
. Hence, under the condition
(\ref{Cdt;D=P})
, and the smallness assumption on both
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
, we deduce that the dielectric resonance equals the plasmonic resonance up to an additive small error term. To put it simply, a hybrid dielectric-plasmonic dimer can have a common resonance.

7.2.
Justification of
(\ref{SL1})-(\ref{SL4})
.

The calculation has been divided into four parts.

(1)

Computation of
{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}
.

{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(1)}\,:=\,\int_{B_{1}}\phi_{n_{0},B_{1}}(x)\,dx\,\otimes\,\int_{B_{1}}\phi_{n_{0},B_{1}}(x)\,dx\,=\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,I_{3},

where we have assumed that
B_{1}
is being the unit ball, i.e.,
B_{1}\equiv B(0,1)
, and
n_{0}\,=\,1
, see
[
11
, Section 3.1, Formula (3.1)]
.

(2)

Computation of
{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}
.

{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\,:=\,\sum_{n}\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx,

which by using the fact that

\int_{B_{2}}\phi_{n,B_{2}}(x)\,dx\,=\,-\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\,dx,

we get

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(1)}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\,dx\,\otimes\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n}\,\langle Q,e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\otimes\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot\sum_{n}\,\langle Q,e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{2})}\,\otimes\,e_{n,B_{2}}^{(1)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{B_{2}}Q(x)\cdot\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Q\right)(x)\,dx.

(3)

Computation of
{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}
.

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})}\,\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{1}}e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle I_{3},e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes\int_{B_{1}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(B_{1})}\,e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{B1EigFct})}{=}

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle I_{3},e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes\int_{B_{1}}\nabla M_{B_{1}}^{-1}\left(e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{B_{1}}\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle I_{3},e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes\nabla M_{B_{1}}^{-1}\left(e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{B_{1}}\,\nabla M_{B_{1}}^{-1}\left(\sum_{n}\,\left\langle I_{3},e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{1})}\otimes e_{n,B_{1}}^{(3)}\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{B_{1}}\,\nabla M_{B_{1}}^{-1}\left(I_{3}\,\chi_{B_{1}}\right)(x)\,dx.

Besides, in the case where
B_{1}
is the unit ball, i.e.,
B_{1}\equiv B(0,1)
, we know that

\nabla M_{B_{1}}\left(I_{3}\right)\,=\,\frac{1}{3}\,I_{3},\quad\text{in}\;B_{1},

see
[
20
, Formula (1.17)]
. Hence,

{\bf P}_{0,1}^{(2)}\,=\,\int_{B_{1}}\,3\,I_{3}\,dx\,=\,3\,\left|B_{1}\right|\,I_{3}\,=\,4\,\pi\,I_{3}.

(4)

Computation of
{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}
.

{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\,:=\,\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{\star},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\otimes\int_{B_{2}}e_{n_{\star},B_{2}}^{(3)}(x)\,dx.

By letting
B_{2}
to be the unit ball, i.e.
B_{2}\,=\,B(0,1)
, and the index
n_{\star}\,=\,1
, we obtain

{\bf P}_{0,2}^{(2)}\,=\,\frac{4\,\pi}{27}\,I_{3},

see for instance
[
19
, subsection 4.5.3]
.
